# Platform Surface Inventory

Status: current
Audience: platform operator, runtime maintainer, security maintainer
Owner: platform maintainers
Evidence: docs/generated/inventory/platform-surfaces.json; docs/config/platform-surface-map.json
Source revision: `0a716c55977a3fba651a728371720641f80f95b4`
Applies to: dependencies, runtime resources, secret references, server endpoints, outbound connections, stores, events, and Ops MCP tools

Last verified: 2026-09-21

This reference lists platform surfaces and their activation state. A source link identifies the maintained declaration; it does not prove live availability.

Dependency classes have precise meanings. `current-profile-required` is required by the checked-in complete profile. `enabled-feature-required` is required only while that enabled feature is used. `operation-conditional` is required by the named operation. `supply-chain-required` supplies selected runtime images. `configured-but-unresolved` has a concrete service or readiness signal but lacks one complete selected client contract. `optional-platform` adds a platform capability without deciding pipeline success. `planned` has acceptance conditions but no supported runtime path.

## Runtime Signals

An `active` signal belongs to a selected profile. A `conditional` signal requires its stated option or operation. An `inactive` signal belongs to an unselected profile, and `fixture-only` identifies non-runtime render scaffolding. A signal does not by itself prove a successful live connection.

## Render Profile Selection

Profile state distinguishes maintained selected renders from opt-in comparison renders. An inactive comparison render contributes only conditional resources and signals.

| Profile | Selected state | Activation condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `helm:nova` | `active` | selected maintained profile |
| `helm:buster` | `active` | selected maintained profile |
| `helm:prism-agent` | `active` | selected maintained profile |
| `helm:prism` | `active` | selected maintained profile |
| `helm:ops-default` | `active` | selected maintained profile |
| `helm:ops-tailscale` | `inactive` | the selected Ops default is tailscale.enabled=false; deploy-ops-pod.sh opts in only when OPS_TAILSCALE_AUTHKEY_FILE is supplied |

## Runtime Resources

Cluster-scoped objects use `cluster`. Helm lifecycle Jobs are active only during the named hook phase. An inactive or conditional profile does not describe a currently selected resource.

## HTTP Endpoints

`active` routes are admitted by the selected runtime. `conditional` routes also require the stated listener or feature switch. `ANY` means the handler does not restrict the method at this routing boundary.

## Outbound HTTP Connections

`active` calls can be made by the selected runtime. `conditional` calls also require the stated feature or operation.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

## State And Cache Names

These identities include persistent stores and explicitly named ephemeral caches from declared state-owning runtime sources. Kubernetes claims appear in Runtime Resources.

## Store Policies

Choose the actual store instance and its caller before interpreting a limit. A fallback applies only when the input is absent; a configured expression is not an observed effective value. Capacity admission, lease expiry, pending-evidence reclamation, domain retirement, backup freshness and physical-media deletion are different operations.

The source tables identify actual declarations and rules without running them. A selected chart value does not prove upstream enforcement or a live mount. An owner with no general deletion method does not establish that no external cleanup tool exists. Follow the linked operating explanation and stop when ownership or quiescence is uncertain.

These configured or dynamic path families do not become one shared store merely because their implementation uses the same filename.

| Instance | Path selection | Actual policy | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ` worker-native-stdout-stderr ` | selected NativeAttemptJournal.outputRoot(envelope)/stdout and /stderr | [worker-native-output-spool](#worker-native-output-spool) | Original admitted Worker host output, not every internal temp file. |

| ` buster-report-adapter-source-copy ` | observabilityRoot/report-adapters/adapter-UUID | [buster-evidence-and-report-snapshots](#buster-evidence-and-report-snapshots) | Selected report-adapter execution source snapshot; classify transient/uncertain-cleanup not another durable application DB. |
| ` configured-state-store ` | Selected state-store context.config.root -> records/store.json | [common-state-store-instance](#common-state-store-instance) | Supported public adapter configuration/capability store instance, not generic everypath expansion. |
| ` configured-telemetry-store ` | Selected telemetry-store context.config.root -> records/store.json | [common-telemetry-store-instance](#common-telemetry-store-instance) | Supported public adapter configuration/capability store instance, not generic everypath expansion. |
| ` configured-wait-store ` | Selected wait-store context.config.root -> records/store.json | [common-wait-store-instance](#common-wait-store-instance) | Supported public adapter configuration/capability store instance, not generic everypath expansion. |
| ` configured-artifact-store ` | Selected artifact-store context.config.artifactRoot -> records/store.json plus blobs/sha256/HH/rest | [common-artifact-store-instance](#common-artifact-store-instance) | Supported public adapter configuration/capability store instance, not generic everypath expansion. |
| ` configured-operator-messaging ` | Selected operator-messaging context.config.deliveryRoot -> records/store.json | [common-operator-messaging-instance](#common-operator-messaging-instance) | Supported public adapter configuration/capability store instance, not generic everypath expansion. |
| ` configured-transport-publisher ` | Selected transport-publisher context.config.deliveryRoot -> records/store.json | [common-transport-publisher-instance](#common-transport-publisher-instance) | Supported public adapter configuration/capability store instance, not generic everypath expansion. |

### nova-run-hash-journals

Owner: Nova Core maintainers; plugin Foundation/SDK maintainers for shared persistence

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | append-only authority |
| Path and instance | Run identity selects the verified hashed or compatible legacy run root. Journals store events, effects, signals, administrative decisions and observer delivery/checkpoint history. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Successful journal records remain; writer-lock contention timeout is not record retention. |
| Cleanup trigger | Journal opening may truncate only an incomplete uncommitted tail; successful entries are not age-pruned by this class. |
| Failure and stop condition | Corrupt committed history, rewind/divergence or unresolved external outcome blocks recovery rather than deleting evidence. |
| Retained authority | Retain hash history together with snapshots, referenced results and related adapter state. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#distinguish-retirement-from-arbitrary-cleanup).

### nova-run-snapshot

Owner: Nova Core maintainers; plugin Foundation/SDK maintainers for shared persistence

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | immutable recovery snapshot |
| Path and instance | The selected run root contains the canonical run snapshot; other snapshot names are conflict/legacy guards, not current independent writers. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | The published original snapshot remains recovery authority. |
| Cleanup trigger | Exclusive atomic link publishes the snapshot and finally removes only its own staging name. |
| Failure and stop condition | Existing snapshot authority rejects replacement; publication failure may precede the final cleanup. |
| Retained authority | Retain graph, registry and pinned runtime profiles for the original run. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#distinguish-retirement-from-arbitrary-cleanup).

### nova-effect-result-blobs

Owner: Nova Core maintainers; plugin Foundation/SDK maintainers for shared persistence

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | externalized effect authority |
| Path and instance | Large effect results are content-addressed siblings of the effects journal; audit summaries are separate projections. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Externalization threshold changes representation, not store capacity or expiry. |
| Cleanup trigger | Atomic staging/link cleanup removes temporary names, not committed result blobs; no general blob-deletion method is exposed here. |
| Failure and stop condition | Missing or changed referenced bytes prevent hydration; a completed event does not authorize their deletion. |
| Retained authority | Retain referenced result bytes with effect request, acceptance and completion history. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#distinguish-retirement-from-arbitrary-cleanup).

### nova-resource-lock-metadata

Owner: Nova Core maintainers; plugin Foundation/SDK maintainers for shared persistence

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | ownership coordination plus fencing history |
| Path and instance | Resource identities map to active owner/claim directories and a shared persistent lock journal. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Lease expiry and provisioning timeouts regulate authority, not journal-history retention. |
| Cleanup trigger | Release appends its released transition before owned active-directory removal. Dead or absent-owner reclaim first renames the active directory, then attempts an expired transition only when owner metadata exists, and removes the renamed directory in finally. |
| Failure and stop condition | Reclaim can remove the renamed active directory even when appending the expiry transition fails; missing owner metadata emits no expiry transition. A live owner blocks acquisition, and expiry or directory absence never proves external work stopped. |
| Retained authority | Retain prior fencing tokens and lock history needed to reject stale operations. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#distinguish-retirement-from-arbitrary-cleanup).

| Selected authority | Role | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ` nova-resource-lock-metadata-authority-1 ` | effect implementation | [skills/nova/core/effects/locks.ts:42](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/0a716c55977a3fba651a728371720641f80f95b4/skills/nova/core/effects/locks.ts#L42-L42) |
| ` nova-resource-lock-metadata-authority-2 ` | selected caller TTL fallback and storage root | [skills/nova/core/execution/engine-runtime.ts:75](https://github.com/datrab/kubeclaw/blob/0a716c55977a3fba651a728371720641f80f95b4/skills/nova/core/execution/engine-runtime.ts#L75-L75) |

### foundation-records

Owner: plugin Foundation/SDK maintainers

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | bounded durable records with explicit retirement |
| Path and instance | The selected caller root contains a canonical record snapshot and its writer-lock metadata. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Record count, payload size and serialized state admission limits reject new work rather than evict records. |
| Cleanup trigger | Explicit owner/digest-selected authorized retirement removes only payloads and retains tombstones and receipts. |
| Failure and stop condition | Ambiguous ownership, changed payload or no net capacity gain rejects retirement. |
| Retained authority | Retain idempotency headers, sequence continuity, retirement identity and source-domain evidence. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#distinguish-retirement-from-arbitrary-cleanup).

### worker-native-attempt-journal

Owner: Worker Core maintainers; selected native host/source-component maintainer

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | retained native attempt authority |
| Path and instance | The selected caller journal root separates metadata, input/result blobs, original outputs and bounded striped coordination. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Admission reserves retained bytes, crash leftovers, pending commitments and future output/result/state overhead. |
| Cleanup trigger | New expired work is rejected before retaining new input; historical replay remains available and completed attempts are not automatically deleted. |
| Failure and stop condition | Capacity or persistence/ownership uncertainty fences admission rather than pruning authoritative attempts. |
| Retained authority | Retain accepted input, original output and sealed result beyond the serving process. |

### prism-nonce

Owner: Prism worker authentication receiver

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | opportunistic-expiry |
| Path and instance | Replay identity is audience plus nonce digest in the PostgreSQL nonce table. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Expired rows may be removed during authenticated nonce consumption; this is not a background sweep. |
| Cleanup trigger | Valid timestamp, nonce and HMAC lead to consume, which deletes a bounded expired batch before conflict-protected insert. |
| Failure and stop condition | Database admission/deadline/query failures reject authentication. Readiness check alone does not delete rows. |
| Retained authority | Never discard unexpired replay authority or treat a cleanup attempt as a complete sweep. |

### prism-quarantine

Owner: Prism ingestion and Control

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | ttl-and-explicit-release |
| Path and instance | Configured quarantine root contains digest-named acquisition bytes. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Only acquired quarantine content is subject to mtime-based expiry; logs, reports and corpus rows are not expired by this timer. |
| Cleanup trigger | Startup reaper and unref timer remove eligible digest names; authenticated acquisition DELETE releases a selected object. |
| Failure and stop condition | Reaper logs periodic failures; explicit DELETE ignores already-absent object only. Control keeps corpus restricted until cleanup succeeds and preserves publication/cleanup failures. |
| Retained authority | Keep actual acquisition/publication identity; repeated exclusive-create collision does not refresh file mtime. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#paths-are-formulas-tied-to-an-actual-configuration).

### prism-engine-cache

Owner: Prism engine

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | ram-eviction |
| Path and instance | Pending and completed maps hold process-local execution data. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Completed entries obey independent count and serialized-byte ceilings; cache is not durable operation authority. |
| Cleanup trigger | Completed hit refreshes order; retention evicts oldest entries to fit, skips oversized results and rejects excessive in-flight work. |
| Failure and stop condition | Invalid configured limits, owner conflicts or cancellation reject; restart loses cache only. |
| Retained authority | Control retains durable request/result authority. |

Operating procedure: [explanation](stores-and-retention.md#capacity-is-not-one-universal-byte-limit).

### prism-backups

Owner: Prism backup owner

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | capacity-admission-not-pruning |
| Path and instance | Backup root stores immutable completed groups and visibly incomplete attempts. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Media capacity admission is separate from Job history and finished-Job TTL; no automatic group pruning exists. |
| Cleanup trigger | Scheduled backup/verify/database-proof commands use the same bounded native shell owner. |
| Failure and stop condition | Insufficient retained capacity refuses rather than deleting previous groups; dump/copy failure retains incomplete bytes. |
| Retained authority | Database dump precedes immutable artifact superset; keep external credentials and independent failure-domain copy. |

### litellm-backups

Owner: LiteLLM PostgreSQL recovery owner

| Policy duty | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Kind | freshness-and-capacity-not-pruning |
| Path and instance | Selected backup root is distinct from the application database volume. |
| Retention and capacity boundary | Maximum age bounds accepted backup freshness, not media deletion; completed groups are never automatically retired. |
| Cleanup trigger | Scheduled mode verifies selected backup or creates and verifies a new group. |
| Failure and stop condition | Retained capacity refuses with incomplete groups preserved; restore requires matched binding and a different empty database. |
| Retained authority | Keep database/application image/version and external encryption/role credentials; restore still needs application acceptance. |

### kubernetes-pvc-csi
